$host = strtolower(explode(':', $_SERVER['HTTP_HOST'] ?? '', 2)[0]);
$cli_hosts = json_decode('["p.1-s.es", "p.1-s.eu", "pxe.1-s.es", "pxe.1-s.eu"]', true);
if (in_array($host, $cli_hosts, true)) { readfile(__DIR__ . '/cli.html'); exit; }
$arc_cat = __DIR__ . '/catalog.json';
$dat_cat = is_file($arc_cat) ? json_decode(file_get_contents($arc_cat), true) : array('resources' => array());
if (!is_array($dat_cat)) { $dat_cat = array('resources' => array()); }
$dat_cat += array('base_url' => 'https://aux.1-s.es', 'pxe_url' => 'http://pxe.1-s.es');
?>

      <section class="contents-section" id="contents" aria-labelledby="contents-title"><div><div class="eyebrow">EXCALIBUR / CANADA</div><h2 id="contents-title">Open the image. Get the file.</h2><p>Browse the original folders of deployed ISOs. Download a kernel, initrd, SquashFS or any listed file directly, without fetching the complete image.</p><p>Directories and file links are generated from the deployed trees. Also works in Links, Lynx and an SSH terminal.</p></div><a class="button button-outline" href="contents.html">Browse deployed files <span>↗</span></a></section>
      <section class="network-section" id="network"><div><div class="eyebrow">SKIP THE USB</div><h2>The network is<br>the starting line.</h2><p>Start an iPXE client, request an address and load the menu from the boot server. The generated menu adapts to the client architecture and firmware.</p><a class="text-link" href="http://pxe.1-s.es/#network" data-open-boot>Open the network boot guide <span>↗</span></a></div><div class="network-steps"><div><span>01</span><section><h3>Connect the client</h3><p>Start iPXE from a network card, a USB key or an existing bootloader.</p></section></div><div><span>02</span><section><h3>Load the boot menu</h3><p>Use DHCP, then chain the generated <code>menu.ipxe</code> from the mirror.</p></section></div><div><span>03</span><section><h3>Choose a fresh start</h3><p>Only local payloads with a supported recipe and required files appear in the generated menu.</p></section></div></div></section>
      <section class="deployment-section" id="deployment"><div class="section-heading"><div><div class="eyebrow">INDEPENDENT CLI APPLICATIONS</div><h2>Download, deploy, check, generate.</h2></div><a class="button button-outline" href="downloads/aux-fast-boot.zip" download>Download command bundle <span>↓</span></a></div><div class="deployment-grid"><div class="deployment-copy"><p>Select resources and destinations in <code>catalog.json</code>, download and deploy independently, then run <code>checkstatus</code>. The generator uses that inventory for catalogs, the iPXE menu and sitemaps. Paths, budgets and Bastion directories belong in <code>fast-boot.json</code>.</p><div class="mode-selector" data-enhance hidden role="group" aria-label="Deployment mode"><button data-mode="disk" aria-pressed="false">Disk</button><button data-mode="ram" class="selected" aria-pressed="true">RAM</button><button data-mode="ftp" aria-pressed="false">FTP</button></div><h3 id="mode-title">Explicit RAM copy</h3><p class="mode-description" id="mode-description">Select RAM in the catalogue policy. Review the plan, copy prepared resources into tmpfs, then inspect their state before publication. Check available memory and the configured budget.</p><a class="text-link" href="documentation.html">Read the AUX 8 manual <span>↗</span></a></div><div class="deployment-code"><div class="code-label"><span>BASH / LINUX</span><button data-enhance hidden id="copy-deploy" aria-label="Copy deployment command">Copy <span>▢</span></button></div><pre id="deploy-command"># Review catalogue policies and each plan first
./aux download --apply
./aux deploy ram --apply
./aux checkstatus
./aux generate --apply</pre><div class="generated-files"><span>GENERATES</span><code>index.php</code><code>menu.ipxe</code><code>sitemaps.xml</code></div></div></div></section>
      <section class="contents-section" id="iso-chapter"><div><div class="eyebrow">OPEN DOCUMENTATION / AUX RESCUE</div><h2>Componer la suite. Preparar el instalador.</h2><p>Elegir tareas de Debian, entornos y grupos de Fedora o colecciones apk de Alpine, con las herramientas AUX adicionales. La selección sirve para instalar una base, ampliar un sistema o personalizar una ISO. Las imágenes nuevas requieren construcción y prueba en el equipo de trabajo.</p></div><a class="button button-outline" href="installer.html">Selector de instalación <span>↗</span></a></section>
      <section class="files-section" id="assistance-keys" aria-labelledby="assistance-keys-title"><div class="section-heading"><div><div class="eyebrow">ASISTENCIA / LLAVES AUX</div><h2 id="assistance-keys-title">Nuestras llaves de asistencia.</h2></div><a class="text-link" href="work.html">Entorno Live y área de trabajo <span>↗</span></a></div><p>Medios AUX propios para diagnóstico, recuperación y asistencia. Las llaves USB, los soportes SSD y su entorno Live forman parte del capítulo de Asistencia.</p><div class="file-list"><a href="downloads/usb/aux-ipxe-usb.img" download><span class="file-icon">USB</span><span><strong>aux-ipxe-usb.img</strong><small>BIOS / x86_64 UEFI · GRUB 5s, DHCP, iPXE · unsigned UEFI</small></span><span class="file-type">IMG</span><span class="file-arrow">↓</span></a><a href="usb-guide.html"><span class="file-icon">USB</span><span><strong>Llave de asistencia USB</strong><small>Preparar el medio AUX y revisar el plan</small></span><span class="file-arrow">↗</span></a><a href="next-key.html"><span class="file-icon">SSD</span><span><strong>Llave / SSD de asistencia</strong><small>Soporte, persistencia y área de trabajo</small></span><span class="file-arrow">↗</span></a></div></section>
      <section class="files-section" id="project-files"><div class="section-heading"><div><div class="eyebrow">TAKE IT WITH YOU</div><h2>Open files.   <dialog data-enhance hidden id="boot-dialog" aria-labelledby="boot-title"><div class="dialog-head"><span class="eyebrow">NETWORK BOOT</span><button class="close-dialog" data-close-dialog aria-label="Close network boot guide">×</button></div><h2 id="boot-title">Start with iPXE.</h2><p>Deploy the files and generate the menu on the mirror first. These commands load the menu hosted at the configured boot server.</p><label class="field-label" for="boot-platform">Client firmware</label><select id="boot-platform"><option value="uefi">x86_64 UEFI</option><option value="bios">Legacy BIOS</option><option value="arm">ARM64 UEFI</option></select><div class="boot-guide"><h3 id="boot-method-title">Start an x86_64 UEFI iPXE client</h3><p id="boot-method-description">Use an iPXE EFI build with the network driver and HTTP support for the target machine. Then enter the following commands at its prompt.</p></div><div class="dialog-code"><pre id="boot-command">dhcp
chain http://pxe.1-s.es/menu.ipxe</pre><button data-enhance hidden data-copy-boot>Copy commands <span>▢</span></button></div><p class="dialog-note">Secure Boot needs a trusted, signed boot chain. Boot recipes are checked for local files; hardware and firmware boot tests remain separate.</p><div class="dialog-links"><a href="downloads/menu.ipxe" download>Download initial menu <span>↓</span></a><a href="https://ipxe.org/download" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">iPXE documentation <span>↗</span></a></div></dialog>
